# Plan 0099 readings log

> Written by `dev`. Raw facts only: readings with machine, adapter, resolution, scene, load and
> commit; gate output counts and wall times; attempts and what each read; resume notes for the
> next agent. No conclusions. The plan's `## Implementation log` keeps one row per phase and the
> close block, and points here.

## Phase 1: The step's split on the line, and the base readings

**Machine and conditions.** The dev box (RTX 3080 Laptop + AMD Cezanne iGPU, 8 cores / 16
threads, max 4.68 GHz: the CPU clock cap was lifted on 2026-11-24 evening; Plan 0098's
inherited dig figures were read at a 3.3 GHz cap), mains. Three lanes ran (Plans 0099, 0100,
0102): every reading took the exclusive perf lock (`/home/igor/Work/voxel-engine/.git/svet-perf.lock`)
for its own run, 6 GiB cap, default nice; the 1-minute load is logged at the lock and at the
end of each run. **A row whose load at the lock is 2 or over is loaded (indicative); under 2 is
quiet at the start.** Most rows are loaded: the other two lanes were compiling and reading
through the night. Adapters: the fly-through by `--adapter RTX` / `--adapter RENOIR` (its
first adapter whose name contains the word; the log's `headless: adapter` line reads "NVIDIA
GeForce RTX 3080 Laptop GPU (Vulkan), driver NVIDIA 615.78.08" and "AMD Radeon Graphics (RADV
RENOIR) (Vulkan), radv Mesa 26.2.5"); the dig rig by `SVET_TEST_ADAPTER=rtx|radeon`.

**Binaries.** `live/bin/p1/flythrough` and `live/bin/p1/gpu_outdoor` (release), built from
this phase's tree before its last clippy pass (that pass renamed `QueueRun::push_frame`'s
arguments and rewrote two expressions; no behaviour moved).

**What the split counts** (`crates/svet-render/src/bake/stages.rs`): a thread-local tally of
the time in `wgpu` calls and the bytes written, read at each stage lap. `render` is every
`write_buffer` (the store's block, table, header and parent writes; the job uniform and faces;
the candidate tables with their build, `JobLoop::refresh_tables`; the parameters), the encode
from the staging buffer's pick to `map_async` with the submit, the device polls (the step's
`Poll` and the job loop's waits), the mapped read with its copy and the unmap, and the bake's
world bind group in the step's setup. `movable` is the rest of each stage. The placement's and
the summaries' moves between stages carry their render share with them. Bytes by buffer:
`pool` (blocks, shown words, sky-stale marks), `table` (table words, removals, a window move's
header and table), `summary` (parent records), `job` (job uniform, faces, candidate tables,
parameters).

### The fly-through rig

`live/p1-fly.sh NAME CPUS FLAGS...` (the lock, the cap, the load lines), batches
`live/p1-batch1.sh` and `live/p1-batch2.sh`, parsers `live/p1-parse.py` (the table) and
`live/p1-stages.py` (the stage split); logs `live/p1r/*.log`. Headless, `--source outdoor`;
the forest `--spawn forest --trees forest --time 15:00`, the hills `--time 17:00`; RTX
`--adapter RTX --profile floor --render 1920x1080 --frames 3600`, RENOIR `--adapter RENOIR
--profile portable --render 1280x720 --frames 6000`; the window's size `--render 2530x1378`
(RTX, Floor); the `taskset` arm `taskset -c 0-7` (cores 0 to 3 with their siblings: 4 cores,
8 logical CPUs; **the dev box's stand-in for the floor's CPU, not the floor**). In full, for
the RTX forest:

    live/bin/p1/flythrough --headless --source outdoor --spawn forest --trees forest --time 15:00 --adapter RTX --profile floor --render 1920x1080 --frames 3600

From the first world frame (the first `bake queue: at` line): "in view 0" is the first such
line reading `in view 0 waiting`; "drain 0" the first `bake queue slice:` line with 0 in view
waiting for their tier; "residency" the `empty after` line (`-`: not emptied in the run). The
step, `render`, `movable` and bytes are the teardown's `frames: queue step B:` line (regime B).
The walls and frame CPU while in view waits and after are the new `frames: queue split:` line
(frames filed by the drain flag of the queue's last step). Drawn / granted is the slice lines'
drawn p50 over the granted slice on `drain` lines, p50 (min).

### The dig base

Rig: `gpu_outdoor`'s `dig_line::reading::dig_timeline` (release `live/bin/p1/gpu_outdoor`) with
the stepper's new device-wait mode (`support::defects::Stepper::wait`: `run` waits on the
device after its submit; `SVET_LINE`'s sixth part `wait`, the default, or `free`). The window's
queue flags (`BakeArgs`' defaults); scene `above` on the RTX (the forest spawn, `--sun 10,180`,
640 x 360 to 960 x 540), `portable` on the RENOIR (eye [31.9, 603.0, 604.3] at 15:00, 853 x 480
to 1280 x 720, Portable's budget, tiers and drain). `live/p1-dig.sh NAME ADAPTER SVET_LINE`,
batch `live/p1-digs.sh` (eight passes; each pass runs, per adapter, waiting A, waiting B,
drained A, drained B: the sequences alternate within a pass), parser `live/p1-digparse.py`
(summary `live/p1-digs-summary.txt`); timelines `live/p1d/*.txt`, logs `live/p1d/*.log`. Each
run its own lock. The D+0 step is `QueueStats::stages` of the dig frame's step; `render` and
`movable` its split; `boxes` and `digs` its laps. The edit wall is new in
`support::defects::dig_along_timed`: the world's op and the frame's `invalidate`, the mirror's
sync between them (a device wait in the rig) not counted; in the rig the step does not run
beside the edit, so no listing thread holds a chunk's read lock while it is timed.

it does so: RTX A 411, 404, 398, 426, 397, 537, 418, 400; RTX B 395, 408, 398, 424, 403, 551,
401, 523; RENOIR A 990, 983, 975, 978, 980, 987, 971, 959; RENOIR B 982, 971, 975, 984, 963,
975, 967, 960 (and 411 on a first RTX A run, `rtx-drained-A-0`). The waiting state digs at
101 in view waiting: RTX after 146 to 179 frames, RENOIR after 340 to 350.

## The merge of main (v0.74.0) into the lane

`bad48346`, `git merge --no-ff main` from `1f2960bf`: no textual conflict (Plan 0102's
upscaler removal and the harness chore's `gpu_settings_rows` and `run/hud_live_tests.rs` beside
Phase 1's queue row). The commit took a bare message (`merge: main into plan-0099`) without the
parenthesis, which a new commit cannot amend. Its checks: build; `node scripts/test-changed.mjs
--since 1f2960bf --profile quick -- --test-threads 2`: 1031 run, 1031 passed, 25 skipped, 98.7 s
(the HUD-live tests among them, with Phase 1's queue row); `--clippy` over the same packages: no
finding outside the next step's then-unused `bake/writes.rs`; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean
on the merge's files; every `scripts/check-*.mjs`: 0. No file had a conflict, so no gpu binary
ran for the merge itself; the next step's gpu run covers the merged tree.

## Phase 2: The queue in two halves, in lockstep

### Step 1 (`39ef163b`, wip): write lists, the fade clock, the mirror's shadow

- `bake/writes.rs`: `Write`, `WriteList`, `ListMaker` (units appended whole, an edit's list,
  refused over-cap units sent as an edit's by the store), `Lists` (the render half: whole lists
  in order under `cap`, edits first and never carried; a first block's landing word written as
  the apply frame plus one and reported), `PoolApplier`. `Store` routes its pool writes through
  `emit` while `Store::out` is set (only inside a queue step); `begin_unit`/`end_unit` group the
  writes of one landing; `Store::landed` patches the copy's landing word.
- The step (`queue/step.rs`): `step` = `step_books` (every stage as before) + `apply_lists`, a
  new 22nd stage `apply` (`STEP_STAGES`), whose `wgpu` share is now where the pool writes'
  render time lands. `take_boxes` runs with the maker's `editing` flag. `Lists::cap` is `None`
  in lockstep, so every list applies in its own frame (the plan's 1 MiB is for the thread).

  `cfg(test)`.

### Step 2 (`f4dc64dd`, `074665fc`, wip): the job loop's render half, the store without a pool, the stamp

- `bake/issue.rs` (new): `GpuRun` (device, queue, mirror, pipeline, groups, params, pool),
  `Prepared` (entry rows, seed, clamp, sky, key, timer), `ReadBack` (words, mapped, wall, GPU
  ms, timer ms), the trait `RenderHalf` (`poll`, `tables`, `refresh_tables`, `write_params`,
  `issue`, `collect`, `cap`, `apply`), `Issuer` (readback buffers, the loop's timer, the GPU's
  in-flight list, the tables' light epoch) and `GpuHalf`, the frame's lockstep half.
  `BakeRun` is the bake half's alone: shadow, world, sky, key. `JobLoop` keeps the books
  (`Planned` entries in flight, `new_tables` for the render half); `dispatch` plans and calls
  `issue`, `collect` takes `ReadBack`s. The calls sit where the unsplit step made them, so
  lockstep bakes what it baked (the gpu run below).
- Item 5: `Store` holds `capacity` and hands every write out; outside a step they go to
  `Store::direct`, which the frame flushes (`BakeLink::flush`) after `bake_until`,
  `start_bake_queue`, `rebake_digs`, `set_missing_light`. The pool buffer is `BakeLink::pool`.
- Item 1 (done-when 2), **read before building**: today a block is thrown away only for an edit
  in its ring while its job is pending or open (`stale`), or a chunk gone from the mirror. Built
  as: a job takes the newest message's frame as it joins the loop (`JobLoop::stamps`; lockstep:
  the step number); `stale` holds the message frame of the edit; a result is released when that
  frame is later than the job's stamp. In lockstep every message is read before any job of that
  step joins, so the comparison discards exactly what the set did. The stamp is the **job's**
  (its listing), not the submit's: a job listed at 2, planned at 4, with an edit at 3 is staled
  today and stays staled; the done-when's cases (planned 4, change 5; stamped 5, change 6 or 4)
  read the same under either. Mirror changes other than an edit's and an eviction do not stale
  (reading B of "a change", which would alter lockstep, is not built).
- Tests (CPU, `bake/queue/split_tests.rs`, a three-chunk slab driven through the bake half's
  step by a recording render half): `a_change_after_a_submit_stales_its_result_and_one_before_does_not`
  (with the stale rule disabled it fails: checked), `a_dig_is_served_whole_in_the_edits_step`.

### Step 3 (`58bc3365`, `f0d06f1a`): the frame message as one owned value

- `bake/queue/message.rs` (new): `FrameMessage` (the step's start, the view, the mirror's
  notes, sky and key, `StepSettings`, the arrivals), `Residency` (owned: the streamer's
  `full_chunks` snapshot or a rule of the caller's), `Inbox` (the render half's mailbox) and
  the step's intake (`take_message`). `BakeQueue::step` takes the shadow, the world, the store,
  the message and the render half; it takes the arrivals and settings first, then the notes
  into the shadow, then builds `BakeRun` itself. `QueueView` lost its lifetime;
  `QueueView::resident` is `Option<Residency>`.
- Edit boxes (`BakeQueue::invalidate`) and prepared masks (`take_prepared`) go into the inbox in
  arrival order and into the books at the start of the step that carries them, with the same
  code the edit ran (`take_box`: the boxes, the seal's forget, the masks, facelessness, block
  reasons, `stale` at `steps + 1`; `take_masks`). In lockstep the step that takes a box is the
  step that read it before, so the dig path's timing holds.
- **The public reads between an edit and the next step** (`settled`, `passed`, `tracks`,
  `empty`, `boxes_waiting`, `digs_settled`; the frame's `bake_settled` and `bake_passed` go
  through them) consult the inbox: a waiting box reads as a box in the books, and a chunk within
  a voxel of it, or with masks waiting, has no known facelessness, seal or block reason. CPU test
  `an_edit_reads_unsettled_before_the_next_step` compares a waiting queue with an eagerly fed one
  over every chunk within two of the box (and a faceless, blocked neighbour reads unsettled and
  not passed); with the overlay off it fails at the first `settled` comparison (checked).
- What still differs between an arrival and the next step, none of it read by a caller there
  today: the seal's forget reaches the stream workers' shells at the step, not the edit (a
  worker's verdict made in between is dropped by the later point of the order, which is
  conservative); a pending mask's seal verdicts are not read until the step; a mask's classes
  are judged against the step's material table; `stats()`'s hook counters and `shells()` count
  at the step.
- svet-world: `Streamer::full_chunks`, the full tier as a copy-on-write `Arc<HashSet>` kept by
  `set_resident` / `unset_resident` (a snapshot is a reference count; test
  `the_full_set_is_the_full_tier_and_a_snapshot_keeps_its_answers`).
- Not moved: the knob setters (`set_bake_*`) and the app's `bake_queue_mut` writes stay direct
  writes into the queue; they are set at load or by a key, not each step, and carrying the knobs
  whole in each message would undo a direct write a test makes (`edit_ms`, `drain`).
- `f0d06f1a`: `gpu_sun`'s `a_provisional_bake_is_caught_up` failed at `58bc3365` (0 catch-ups
  of 1) because the converted rig took the world's chunks once, before the slab chunk arrives
  mid-run; the rig now snapshots them each step, as the borrowed rule read them live.

### Phase 3 gotchas (from Phase 2)

- A fade's later `show` of a first block whose landing list is not yet applied carries the bake
  half's landing word, and would overwrite the render half's. Lockstep is unaffected (one
  frame). Either mark every block write of a chunk whose landing word is unconfirmed, or have
  the render half keep the word by chunk until the bake half confirms.
- The stamp in lockstep is the step number; on the thread it must be the newest message's
  frame, and `take_box`'s `steps + 1` becomes the box's message frame.
- The inbox lives on `BakeQueue` so the public reads can consult it in lockstep. On the thread
  the inbox stays with the render half and the reads go through the bake half: they must count
  the messages in flight (a box sent but not yet taken) as the inbox does now. The knob setters
  and `bake_queue_mut`'s direct writes must go through the message too.
- One-shot runs make their own `Issuer` and an empty `Lists`; their writes are direct and
  flushed by the frame after the call (the bake shader reads no pool, so the order is safe).
- A ring-1 job's table is written at the next `dispatch` (`JobLoop::new_tables`), not at its
  open; nothing reads the slot before then.
- `Lists::take` refuses a landing list over the cap; `ListMaker` never makes one.

## Architect's code reading: the RENOIR's ring-1 landing on a drained queue (2026-11-25)

> Written by the architect, not `dev`: Phase 1's done-when (6) sent the result here. Read from
> the code at `1f2960bf` and from Phase 1's timelines (`live/p1d/*.txt`); nothing was built or
> run. Each claim says whether it is read from code, read from a log, or derived.

**The result.** RENOIR, drained, sequence A: ring 1 at D+1 for dig 1 and D+3 or D+4 for digs 2
and 3 in 15 of 16 (D+2 once). The same digs read D+2 and D+1 on the waiting RENOIR and on the
RTX in both states.

### 1. What a ring-1 job waits on (code)

- **The edit.** `take_boxes` (`bake/queue/digs.rs:24`) withdraws, in the edit's step, every job
  that is not key-only in a ring-1 chunk and **every dig job in any ring chunk** (lines 47 to
  55: `dig_open.contains_key(c) || slots.iter().any(touches)`). A job of another kind in a ring
  chunk the box does not touch is not withdrawn: it is marked stale (line 68) and its block is
  thrown away when it ends (`Placement::place`, `bake/queue/step.rs:536`). So a ring-2 job of
  the previous dig is withdrawn at the next edit if the new ring reaches its chunk.
- **The fade.** Ring 1 never waits for one: `open_digs` filters by `d.near || !fading(c)`
  (`digs.rs:242` to 246). Only a chunk with no ring-1 brick waits out a `DIG_FADE` fade.
- **The slot.** `open_now` (`digs.rs:392`) opens ring 1 into a free slot in the edit's step, and
  withdraws the last open non-dig job where none is free. A withdrawn job with rounds in flight
  keeps its slot until they are read back (`JobLoop::withdraw`, `bake/job_loop.rs:213` to 217).
- **The submit.** One dispatch a step, while fewer than `IN_FLIGHT` (2) are unread
  (`step.rs:382`); ring 1 is promoted, so `plan_rounds` (`job_loop.rs:397`) gives it the cap
  first. **The cap** is `open_step`'s (`step.rs:223` to 228):
  `(slice + edit_ms while a dig waits or bakes) * 1e6 / ns_per_sample`, clamped to 16 384 and
  `SUBMIT_SAMPLES`. `ns_per_sample` is `0.8 * old + 0.2 * (gpu ms / samples)` of the submits
  read back in a step with at least 4096 samples (`step.rs:417` to 422).
- **The read-back.** `map_async` at the submit (`job_loop.rs:595`); the step's `Poll`
  (`step.rs:272`) and `collect` with `unread = IN_FLIGHT` take what is mapped and wait for
  nothing. The block is placed by the sink in that `collect`.

### 2. What the timelines show (log)

Every drained RENOIR row at and after a dig reads `free 13` or `14`, `unread 1`, `withdrawn 0`:
a slot is free, a dispatch goes out every step, and each submit is read back the next step.
Dig 2 comes 47 to 60 frames after dig 1 and dig 3 one frame after dig 2; dig 1's whole ring has
landed by D+12 to D+19 in seven runs (D+70 once, where the edit withdraws it). Nothing of dig 1
is in the way. What cuts the job is the cap, run 1 (`radeon-drained-A-1.txt`):

    62 D2+0  cap  73691 sent  73735 | flight r1  73735   ([0, 9, 9]: left 147860 of 221595)
    63 D3+0  cap  72826 sent  72840 | flight r1  72840   (re-opened: 225784 and 49238 in [0, 9, 8])
    64 D3+1  cap  72826 sent  72837 | flight r1  72837
    65 D3+2  cap  82884 sent  82937 | flight r1  82937
    66 D3+3  cap  91209 sent  91243 | flight r1  46408 other 44835

Four submits carry dig 3's 275 022 samples; the first chunk's ring 1 is read back at frame 66
(dig 2's D+4), the second's at 67 (dig 3's D+4). Dig 1 in the same run: `cap 278389`, ring 1
180 613, one submit, D+1.

The cap at each dig's frame, and the estimate it implies (`4.0 ms / cap` drained; the slice is
Portable's line 1.0 ms plus `edit_ms` 3.0):

| RENOIR drained A, run | dig 1 cap (ns) | dig 2 cap (ns) | dig 3 cap (ns) | ring 1 lands |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 278 389 (14.4) | 73 691 (54.3) | 72 826 (54.9) | 1, 4, 4 |
| 2 | 363 196 (11.0) | 102 859 (38.9) | 92 691 (43.2) | 1, 4, 3 |
| 3 | 381 143 (10.5) | 85 757 (46.6) | 96 821 (41.3) | 1, 4, 3 |
| 4 | 249 276 (16.0) | 108 377 (36.9) | 111 034 (36.0) | 1, 4, 3 |
| 5 | 297 544 (13.4) | 124 067 (32.2) | 116 061 (34.5) | 1, 3, 3 |
| 6 | 323 755 (12.4) | 76 828 (52.1) | 76 880 (52.0) | 1, 4, 4 |
| 7 | 380 284 (10.5) | 100 029 (40.0) | 94 388 (42.4) | 1, 4, 3 |
| 8 | 358 716 (11.2) | 136 598 (29.3) | 143 403 (27.9) | 1, 3, 2 |

Ring 1 is 180 613, 221 595 and 275 022 samples (dig 3's over two chunks). Each landing is the
submits `ceil(samples / cap)` takes plus one frame; run 8's larger cap is the one D+2. Against
it: RENOIR waiting A, cap 635 349 to 1 025 270 at the digs (9.0 ms: the drain's 6.0 plus 3.0);
RTX drained A 2 063 249 to 4 160 939 and waiting 2 685 274 to 4 721 075, ring 1 115 052 to
246 497. Neither is cut. RENOIR drained B (digs eight frames apart) keeps a cap of 215 000 to
360 000 in run 1 because the previous dig's ring 2 is still drawing large submits: 45 of 48 at D+1.

Between dig 1 and dig 2 of run 1 the cap falls from 236 099 (D1+19, the last ring frame) to
58 313 the next frame (the 3.0 ms goes) and then to 18 000 to 22 000 over 25 frames, with one
job open and one submit a frame.

### 3. Why the estimate climbs (derived)

Inverting the mean (`m = (ns[t+1] - 0.8 ns[t]) / 0.2` for the submit sent at `t - 1`) gives each
submit's GPU ms from the logged caps, and a line fitted over the samples
(`ms = fixed + per * samples`):

| run | fixed ms | ns a sample | small submits | large submits |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RENOIR drained A 1 | 0.87 | 11.3 | 25 600 samples: 1.14 ms, 44.6 ns each | 271 600: 3.73 ms, 13.7 ns |
| RENOIR drained A 3 | 0.77 | 12.8 | 28 900: 1.10 ms, 37.9 ns | 338 400: 5.03 ms, 14.9 ns |
| RENOIR waiting A 1 | 0.64 | 9.7 | none | 533 000 to 883 000: 10 to 12 ns |
| RTX drained A 1 | 0.37 | 0.98 | none under 520 000 | 3 388 000: 3.83 ms, 1.1 ns |
| RTX waiting A 1 | 0.60 | 0.92 | none | 1 923 000: 2.18 ms, 1.1 ns |

**This is arithmetic on the caps, not a reading of a submit's time** (the rig's row does not
print `QueueStats::samples` and `ms`; Phase 2b (a) reads them). It assumes the slice values
above and that the mean moved every step. It says a dispatch costs the RENOIR about 0.8 ms
before its first sample, so under the 1.0 ms line the mean of ms over samples settles where
`samples * mean = 1.0 ms`, at about 20 000 samples and 50 ns, and the dig's 4.0 ms is then
priced at 50 ns: 80 000 samples, whose cost by the fit is about 1.8 ms, not 4.0. The other
reading of the same caps, that the samples drawn between the digs (the reach ring's re-bakes
the first dig opened) are each dearer, is not excluded by the logs, but ring-1 submits of
73 000 to 83 000 samples invert to 22 to 26 ns and the cap climbs back through ring 2, which is
what a fixed part gives.

On the RTX the same fixed part is under the samples a 1.0 ms line buys (about 0.5 to 0.9
million), and the dig's cap is 12 to 30 times ring 1.

### 4. What the bar measures (code)

`dig_timeline` (`apps/flythrough/tests/gpu_outdoor/dig_line.rs`, `line_text`) reads the
queue's trace after each frame's step: a chunk's ring 1 has landed in the first frame where it
held a live dig job with a ring-1 brick the frame before and holds none now, and does not wait
with a ring-1 brick. That is the step whose `collect` placed the job. D+0 is the frame whose
step read the box. In the device-wait mode `Stepper::run` (`support/defects.rs:59` to 70) waits
on the device after the frame, so a submit is mapped by the next step on both adapters: a frame
is one step and one read-back of latency on each, and the adapters differ only in what a cap
holds. **Sequence A's dig 2 cannot read D+1**: dig 3's box, one frame later, touches the same
chunk, `take_boxes` withdraws dig 2's job, and the chunk's ring 1 lands with dig 3's (D+2 for
dig 2 when dig 3 reads D+1).

### 5. Verdict

- The dig's mechanism (Plan 0098 Phases 16 to 18) works as written. No fix to the jump, the
  withdrawal, the slots or the fade's wait.
- **The cap's pricing is a defect**: a mean cost a sample, taken over submits of one size, is
  used to size a submit of another. It bites where a dispatch's fixed cost is near the slice
  (the RENOIR under its line) and the slice then grows (a dig). In reverse it overdraws: the
  first steps after the view drains are sized at the drain's mean (6.0 ms steady gives about
  14 ns; a 1.0 ms slice then sends about 71 000 samples, about 1.7 ms by the fit).
- The nfr's sentence is a reading that did not cover a drained RENOIR. With honest pricing the
  RENOIR's 4.0 ms buys `(4.0 - 0.8) / 12 ns`, about 260 000 samples, to 330 000 at the waiting
  fit's 9.7 ns: dig 1 and dig 2's rings fit, dig 3's 275 022 is at the edge. Expected after the
  fix: 1, 2, 1 or 1, 3, 2. That is the owner's question 7, not a defect.
- Not this plan's, to the backlog: under the RENOIR's 1.0 ms line about four fifths of the
  slice is the dispatch's fixed part.

### 6. For the plan

Phase 2 is unchanged: the estimate and the cap move to the bake half as they are. Phase 2b (the
plan's amendment of 2026-11-25) reads a submit's ms directly, then splits the estimate. Phase 3's
"base plus 2" is held against lockstep on the same rig and state at its own tip.

## Phase 2b: the cap prices a slice by a dispatch's fixed cost and a sample's cost

Rig: Phase 1's dig rig (`dig_line::reading::dig_timeline`, device-wait stepper, Portable on the
RENOIR at the eye of Phase 1, `above` on the RTX); `live/p2b-dig.sh NAME ADAPTER SVET_LINE`
with `BIN=` the binary, timelines and logs `live/p2bd/`, the batch `live/p2b-digs.sh`, the
parser `live/p2b-digparse.py`, the fit `live/p2b-fit.py`, the replays `live/p2b-sim.py` and
`live/p2b-sim2.py`. Binaries: `live/bin/p2b-a` (the Phase 2 tip plus the row's new columns:
the mean's cap, "before") and `live/bin/p2b-b` (the priced cap, "after"). Each run its own
hold of the exclusive perf lock. Landings are frame counts; the loads are given per run
(most runs loaded: three lanes shared the machine until about 05:00).

### (a) The read-back's ms over its samples

The rig's row now prints `read N in K at X ms` (`QueueStats::samples`, the new
`read_submits`, `ms`) and the price. One drained A run a side, fitted over every read-back
with at least 4096 samples (a dispatch each: ms / K over samples / K):

| run | load at lock | read-backs | fixed ms | ns a sample | R squared | samples |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RENOIR drained A (`radeon-drained-A-a`) | 2.47 (loaded, indicative) | 116 | **0.823** | **12.61** | 0.823 | 17 469 to 331 308 |
| RTX drained A (`rtx-drained-A-a`) | 2.10 (loaded, indicative) | 97 | -0.234 | 1.66 | 0.914 | 479 550 to 3 540 377 |

RENOIR by size: 23 000 samples 1.022 ms (44.5 ns each; the line 1.113), 48 800 1.498 ms
(30.7 ns; 1.438), 196 000 3.654 ms (18.6 ns; 3.299), 319 000 4.373 ms (13.7 ns; 4.848). **The
stop did not fire**: the RENOIR's fit holds a fixed part of 0.82 ms (the architect's arithmetic:
0.77 to 0.87). The RTX's read-backs are all over 480 000 samples and hold no fixed part the
fit can resolve (its intercept is under 0). The done-when (3) floor at this fit:
`0.9 x (4.0 - 0.823) / 12.61 ns` = **226 755** samples.

### (b) The estimate

`SampleCost` (`bake/budget.rs`, no GPU) holds the mean (printed `ns a sample`, as before) and
the price; the queue feeds it each step's timed read-backs (`SubmitDone::timed`, the dispatches
the step read) and sizes the cap by `SampleCost::cap(slice + edit_ms)` =
`(ms - fixed) / ns a sample`, clamped as before (16 384 to `SUBMIT_SAMPLES`).

**Attempt 1 (the amendment's letter): the fixed part kept, the ns a sample re-read.** A fit
over the last 32 read-backs, taken at a spread of 1.25 and R squared 0.8, its intercept
clamped to [0, the least ms]; the ns a sample `(ms - fixed x dispatches) / samples` over the
recent read-backs (0.8 a read-back). One drained A run (`live/p2bd-attempt1/`): ring 1 D+1, 2,
2, but dig 2's cap 217 495 (under 226 755), and the timeline showed why: a fit over the dig
ring's large submits alone (300 000 to 350 000, 3.7 to 6.8 ms) came out under 0 and set the
fixed part to 0 again; then the small submits' ns a sample, read against a fixed part off by a
little, swung 13 to 21 ns (a 20 000-sample submit's 0.4 ms beyond 0.6 ms of fixed carries all
of a 0.1 ms error). Replays of three timelines' read-backs through variants (window 32 to 256,
decay 0.8 or 0.95, reject or clamp the intercept, the slope's standard error, the spread a fit
needs) at dig 2's frame: the amendment's rule gave caps of 148 000 to 245 000; **the slope kept
and the level re-read** gave 261 000 to 277 000 at a 64 read-back window and decay 0.8.

**What landed (a deviation from the amendment's wording, the same two figures):** the slope
of a least-squares line over the last 64 read-backs is the ns a sample, taken when the sizes
spread by 1.25 and by the last taken fit's spread up to 3 (the small submits under a steady
line spread by up to about 2 with the estimate's noise, and their local slope, 17 to 20 ns
here, is not the cost of a grown slice), and its standard error is within 10 % of it. The
fixed part is where a line of that slope through the recent read-backs (0.8 a read-back) meets
no samples, re-read every step; under 0 the price is the recent ms over samples; with no slope
yet the price is the mean (fixed 0). Under a steady slice the cap draws what the mean draws
(the line passes through what it drew). The amendment kept the fixed part and re-read the ns;
this keeps the ns (the slope) and re-reads the fixed part: the small submits read the level
well and the slope badly, the spread read the slope.

## Architect's ruling on Phase 2b (2026-11-25)

> Written by the architect, not `dev`. Read from the code at `30f6b7ea` and from Phase 2b's
> timelines (`live/p2bd/`); nothing was built or run. Each claim says whether it is read from
> code, read from a log, or derived. The rulings are in the plan's amendment of the same name.

### 1. The estimate as built (code, `bake/budget.rs`)

- `read`: the mean as before; the decayed sums `recent` (dispatches, samples, ms; 0.8 a
  read-back); the fit's span of 64. A fit is taken at a spread of 1.25, or of the last taken
  fit's spread up to 3, and kept only if its slope is over 0 and its standard error within 10 %.
  A rejected fit changes nothing: the negative fit of attempt 1 cannot reset anything here.
- `price`: with a slope, `fixed = (ms - slope x samples) / dispatches` over `recent`; over 0
  that is the price, else `(0, ms / samples)` of `recent`; with no slope `(0, mean)`.
- `cap`: `(ms - fixed) / per`, clamped to 16 384 and `SUBMIT_SAMPLES`. A fixed part over the
  slice gives the floor.

**Derived.** At a steady read rate `recent` holds 5 dispatches' weight, so one read-back slower
than the line by `e` ms raises the fixed part by `e / 5` and the excess decays 0.8 a read-back
(a third left after five). An outlier that pushes the fixed part over the slice sends the floor;
the mean's rule under the same outlier (20 000 samples in 20 ms: `0.8 x 50 + 0.2 x 1000` =
240 ns, 16 600 samples for 4.0 ms) sends about the same, so the worst case is not worse. A stale
slope does not move a steady cap (the line passes through what was drawn); it moves a changed
slice's cap by the slope's own error.

**Read (log).** `radeon-drained-B-after-1`, the price at each dig's D+0: 0.140 ms + 10.48 ns,
0.000 + 11.90, 0.644 + 14.40, **1.531 + 14.34**, 1.464 + 14.04, 1.375 + 13.45; run 2: 0.263 +
9.84, 0.365 + 11.67, 0.140 + 11.08, 0.123 + 11.27, 1.259 + 12.25, 1.109 + 12.40; run 3: 0.513 +
9.59, 0.224 + 11.62, 0.897 + 14.25, 0.008 + 13.79, 0.000 + 11.69, 0.795 + 13.08. Run 1's dig 4:
the read-back before it is 36 916 samples in 4.2076 ms (the fit gives 1.29); cap 211 037, ring 1
211 048 + 28 443 = 239 491, under the 252 000 that `(4.0 - 0.823) / 12.61 ns` buys. So that
D+2 is the outlier's. The fixed part swings 0 to 1.5 ms inside one run at loads of 1.4 to 42:
the load and the estimate's noise are not separated by these runs, hence the quiet re-read
(Phase 2c (6)) and its stop at Phase 1's base count.

**Not taken:** clamping the fixed part to the least ms a dispatch in the span. The span holds
read-backs under the fit's fixed part (16 384 samples in 0.4206 ms, `radeon-drained-A-after-2`
frame 91), so the clamp would under-read it and overdraw.

### 2. The first steps after the view drains

The rig's N-2 is the first step after the first drain of the run: a 6.0 ms slice from the first
frame, every submit 300 000 to 450 000. The dev's (a) keeps a fixed part read at small sizes,
(b) the line's last steady cap; at a first drain neither exists, so neither changes N-2. They
would act on a later drain (the view fills again after a move), which no rig here reads.
Bound (derived): the cap priced with no fixed part is `line / mean`, whose true cost is
`line + fixed`; the overdraw is the fixed part, 0.82 ms on the RENOIR, for the read-backs the
level takes to come down (0.8 each). The mean's rule read 62 132 to 84 554 at the same row.
After a dig the same transition is priced right, because the dig's spread is in the span: D3+25
of `radeon-drained-A-after-2` sends the floor at 1.871 ms + 9.99 ns.

### 3. Ring 2 on the drained RENOIR

**Intent.** ADR-0046's outcome: "Rings 1 and 2 re-bake at the queue's settings ahead of every
other job, with the slice raised while they wait"; ring 3 (the light radius, the reach ring)
"opens when a touched brick was lit". ADR-0048: "Digs (rings 1 and 2) stay ahead of every score,
ring 3 scores as a refinement". Plan 0098 Phase 16 made ring 1 a job of its own with a slot at
once; it left ring 2 "as today" and its order test asserts ring 1's rounds only.

**Code.** `open_digs` pushes its jobs to the front of the pending ones and `open_now` opens the
first `first` (the ring-1 jobs) into slots. `promote` sorts `JobLoop::open`. `plan_rounds` plans
`open.iter()` first and opens pending jobs only `while budget.room()`. So a ring-2 job pushed
while an open job that is not a dig's still has a cap's worth to draw is not opened, with
slots free.

**Log** (`radeon-drained-A-after-2`): D3+1 `r1 11430 other 218651`, free 14, pend 5 (all dig
jobs, 49 152 to 176 128 samples each); D3+2 to D3+15 `other` 233 101 to 353 789 a frame, free
15, open 1, pend 6 to 10 (every one a dig job, [0, 9, 9]'s 1 015 808 among them); D3+16 `r2
131791 other 219632`, the other job's last; D3+17 on, ring 2 alone, done at D3+23. Fourteen
frames of the edit's grant went to a job that is not the dig's. Before (`before-2`): caps of
120 000 to 195 000, `other 0` from D3+0 to D3+12, ring 2 in flight from D3+2: no such job was
open, because under the small cap ring 1's samples left were over two slices and the opening
(`samples_left < 2 * cap`) admitted nothing in the dig's step.

**Visible?** The RTX (`rtx-drained-A-after-1`) lands every ring chunk by D+6 to 8 before and
after; the waiting RENOIR D+7 to 9 both. The drained RENOIR's 20 to 26 lies inside the 14 to 31
read before. What is held for those frames is ring 2's own earlier record around the hole, not
the hole's provisional value (ring 1 lands at D+1 or D+2).

### 4. Question 7's figures (derived from (a)'s fit, 0.823 ms + 12.61 ns)

`slice + edit_ms` 4.0 ms buys 251 900 samples; the caps read at digs 2 and 3 were 256 345 to
279 528. A ring of 275 022 costs 4.29 ms, of 330 000 4.98 ms. At `edit_ms` 5 the grant buys
410 500. Option (b) moves dig 3's D+2 (7 of 24) and not dig 2's, which lands with dig 3.

### 5. Phase 3's split

(a), (b) and (c) differ only in whether the render thread waits for the list made from its own
message: never (b), on a frame that carries a box (a), on every frame while a dig waits or
bakes (c). A wait is additive on a channel whose lists name the message they were made from, so
the thread, the inbox and outbox, the stamp, the mailbox, the knobs and the landing word are
common. The default stays `off` in a window through 3a: with it `on` the fly-through would show
(b)'s picture, which is the owner's to pick.

## Phase 3a: the bake half on its own thread (less the dig clause)

### What landed (commits, all on `plan-0099`)

- `dc2394ef` the three routings in lockstep (the render half's mailbox `Inbox`, the message's
  frame as the stamp and the fades' clock, the knobs as `Arrival::Set`, the landing word);
  `53a29dbf` the thread's frame (`bake/thread.rs`, `BakeThread`); `647fcb6f` the wiring
  (`bake/link.rs`, `frame/bake_thread.rs`, `Books` and `QueueRead`'s snapshot reads, the joins);
  `68cacf9f` the setting `RenderSettings::bake_thread`, `--bake-thread on|off`, the banner's
  and the HUD's queue row; `aba47e60` done-when (4) and (12)'s frame half; `0a8e71c8` (5a), a
  waiting box ends the opening (`bake/queue/box_signal.rs`); `99c08238` the snapshot's reach
  read per frame; `e2c269df` the shutdown soak test with the thread on.
- `69fcabf8` the readings below and one fix: the fly-through's once-a-second census request
  went through `bake_queue_mut()`, which is `None` with the thread on, so the `bake queue:`
  line's in-view count (and the HUD's row) froze at the first census and the census stage
  never ran again on the bake thread. It now goes through `Frame::set_bake_queue`, either side.
  Found by the first batch (the RTX thread-on run read `in view 134 waiting` from frame 0 to
  3600); those logs are void (`live/p3ar-void/`).
- Done-whens (1) to (4), (5a), (6) restated, (9) to (12): the CPU tests named in the plan, (4)
  by name on lavapipe (see the gates below). The tests' places: `bake::thread::tests`,
  `bake::queue::split_tests::{routing,thread}`, `bake::writes::tests`,
  `run::hud_live_tests::the_flag_and_the_row_name_the_mode`,
  `gpu_queue::the_threaded_queue_drains_to_the_lockstep_store` (`#[ignore = "soak"]`),
  `gpu_shutdown::the_headless_flythrough_with_the_bake_thread_exits_within_bound`.

### Readings (7) and (8), less the dig

**Rig.** `live/p3a-batch.sh` (eight passes, each RTX off/on then RENOIR off/on, the order
alternating by pass; then the window-size and `taskset` arms, two a side), `live/p3a-fly.sh`
(each run waits up to 15 minutes for the 1-minute load under 2, then the exclusive perf lock
`/home/igor/Work/voxel-engine/.git/svet-perf.lock`, the 16 GiB cap; the load at the lock and at
the end in the log's first and last lines), the binary `live/bin/p3a/flythrough` (release, the
tree of `69fcabf8`), logs `live/p3ar/`, parser `live/p3a-parse.py` (Phase 1's columns plus the
new lines). Two lanes open, the other idle; no live-session runner was up at any stage (checked
before each, and a watcher polled once a minute through the batch): no run was delayed or
voided by a window. Adapters by `--adapter RTX` / `--adapter RENOIR` (the RENOIR on RADV, as
Phase 1). Headless, `--source outdoor --spawn forest --trees forest --time 15:00`; RTX `--profile
floor --render 1920x1080 --frames 7200`, RENOIR `--profile portable --render 1280x720 --frames
6000`; the window's size `--render 2530x1378` (RTX, Floor, 7200); `taskset -c 0-7` (RTX). **The
RTX runs are 7200 frames, not Phase 1's 3600**: with the thread on the frame is about 7.5 ms,
so 3600 frames end before the residency empties; both sides take 7200. 08:27 to 09:36,
2026-11-25.

**What each column is.** Share: the `frames: queue step B:` line's step p50 / p99; with the
thread on that is the render thread's whole wall in `Frame::bake_queue_step` (the message's
build, the take, the submits issued, the lists applied, the read-backs collected, the send),
in lockstep the step's `render` part (its `wgpu` calls; the whole step, all on the render
thread, is given beside it). Walls and CPU while in view waits: the `frames: queue split:`
line. In view 0, drain 0, residency: seconds from the first world frame. Fade share: the new
`frames: fade steps:` line, of the sliced frames whose newest books held a block fading, those
that applied a bake step's output (each step moves every fade by the frames elapsed); lockstep
reads 1 by construction. Snapshot: `frames: bake thread snapshot:` (eye past the reach, the
longest run of consecutive such frames, sky over the floor lost). Render laps: the new
`frames: bake thread render laps:` line.

**Verdicts.**
- **(7), RTX forest, Floor 1080p, quiet, thread on:** share p50 0.582 to 0.608 ms (bar 1.0),
  p99 1.078 to 1.153 ms (bar 4.0): **met in all eight**. Wall p50 while in view waits 7.72 to
  7.94 ms (bar 16.6): **met in all eight** (lockstep, the same batch: 21.15 to 22.21).
- **(7), RENOIR forest, Portable 720p, thread on:** share p50 0.429 to 0.474, p99 0.785 to
  0.836: **met in all eight**. Wall p50 against Phase 1's base (RENOIR forest 2, quiet at the
  start: 33.68 ms while in view waits, 20.79 after): 24.22 to 24.34 while in view waits, 19.07
  to 19.12 after, whole-run 19.15: under the base in every run, so **not over it by more than
  2 %** (against this batch's lockstep: 29.83 and 19.62).
- **The outdoor path's 8.18 ms p99** (`reach-path-rtx`, the first binary, the moving camera)
  is not the forest fresh start's: the forest reads 1.15 at most. No stop. The path was not
  re-read with the laps line.
- **(8) the frame's fps over the drain at the window's size** (RTX, Floor, 2530 x 1378): thread
  on 82 fps while in view waits (12.21 ms), 87 over the run; lockstep 37 (26.77 ms) and 89.
- **(8) the fade share:** RTX forest 0.603 to 0.624 at 134 fps; at the window's size 0.819 to
  0.822 at 87 fps; RENOIR 1.000 at 52 fps; `taskset` 0.626 to 0.638 at 129 fps. Lockstep 1 by
  construction. The plan's guess (at least 90 % at 60 fps) is not read directly: no headless
  run is paced. Derived: a step is taken every 1 / share frames, so the bake thread's period
  is about 7.48 / 0.612 = 12.2 ms on the RTX forest and 11.53 / 0.821 = 14.0 ms at the
  window's size; a 16.7 ms frame then takes a step in nearly every frame. The share falls with
  the frame time, not with the queue.
- **(8) the edit's wait p99:** a dig reading, Phase 3b's.
- **(8) the `taskset` arm:** read above (thread on: share 0.582 / 1.073 and 0.602 / 1.109;
  wall p50 7.72 while in view waits). Its lockstep residency ran 60.6 and 79.5 s; the thread's
  one emptied at 55.9 s and the other not in 7200 frames.
- **(8) the in-view drain**, not expected to shorten: it did, RTX 32.9 to 24.2 s, RENOIR 17.3
  to 12.9 s, the window's size 34.4 to 28.9 s; the residency on the RTX 42.8 to 35.6 s (the
  RENOIR does not empty in 6000 frames on either side, as in Phase 1).
- **Drawn against granted:** the thread's medians 0.960 to 0.980 against lockstep's 0.993 to
  1.007, its minima 0.607 to 0.880 against 0.813 to 0.893 (RTX): the slice is underfed now and
  then (`IN_FLIGHT` spans the round trip; Phase 4's).
- **The snapshot's reach:** every thread-on run read 1 frame past the reach, the longest run
  1 (the first frame, before the first snapshot: its eye is NaN), and 0 frames with the sky
  over the floor lost. The camera is at rest in these runs; the outdoor path's 19 frames past
  (the first binary, no run count) are the moving case.

### The phase's gate (at the tip)

- Quick at `69fcabf8`: 586 run, 586 passed, 24 skipped; `--clippy` 0; fmt 0.
- GPU on lavapipe, exclusive perf lock 09:36:28 (load 1.20) to 09:40:37 (load 4.21): `--profile gpu
  --binaries gpu_queue,gpu_queue_reach,gpu_bake,gpu_sun,gpu_bake_rule,gpu_settings_rows` 86 run, 86
  passed, 31 skipped (232.5 s), and its `sole:` walk 1 passed (15.1 s).
- By name (`--run-ignored all`), lock 09:40:51 (load 3.64, loaded) to 09:42:01 (9.08):
  `the_threaded_queue_drains_to_the_lockstep_store` 15.1 s,
  `the_headless_flythrough_exits_through_every_teardown_stage_within_bound` 60.6 s,
  `the_headless_flythrough_with_the_bake_thread_exits_within_bound` 53.9 s: 3 passed.

### For Phase 2d and Phase 3b

- The setting stays `off` in every profile and in a window; a profile picked in the menu sets
  `bake_thread` back to the profile's `off`, as it does the tiers and the drain. 3b turns the
  window's default `on`.
- With the thread on the render thread's whole share is filed under the `apply` stage
  (`stages.total_ms` and `render_ms[apply]` are its wall in the call; the other stages are the
  bake thread's), so the `queue step` line's step p50 is the render thread's share on either
  side; `frames: bake thread render laps:` splits it (build, take, issue, apply, collect, send).
- `Frame::store()` asserts while the thread runs; `bake_queue_mut()` is `None` then, and
  `QueueRead`'s `Deref` to `BakeQueue` panics: a lockstep-only read. Anything the app asks of
  the queue goes through `Frame::set_bake_queue` (the knob arrives with the next message): the
  census request was the one app path left on `bake_queue_mut()` (fixed at `69fcabf8`); the
  readings' drains (`take_angle_needs`, `listing_census` under `--queue-census`) stay lockstep.
- The books' snapshot carries block words only within 16 voxels of the step's eye (its chunk,
  two under, its summary): an encode whose eye is past that reads no sky over the floor until
  the next snapshot. At rest it read 1 frame past (the first) in every run; the moving camera
  read 19 of 4404 on the outdoor path. Not widened.
- A drained queue still makes one small outbox entry a step (its books' snapshot), which the
  outbox bound counts; the render thread drops the previous snapshot in the take (the take's
  p50 0.19 ms on the RTX forest, its largest lap at p50).
- `fit_store` drops waiting lists when it rebuilds the pool (it joins the thread first); so do
  `start_bake_queue`, `bake_until`, `rebake_digs` and `set_missing_light` join.
- `IN_FLIGHT` spans the round trip with the thread on (issued at the take, mapped a frame or two
  on, read in the next message): drawn against granted dips to 0.61 on the RTX (Phase 4's).
- The fade share falls with the frame time: about 0.61 at 134 fps, 0.82 at 87 fps, 1.0 at 52
  fps (a step every 12 to 14 ms on the RTX). Phase 3b's dig fades (4 frames each) read it.
- A waiting box ends the threaded opening (`box_signal.rs`); the box's own frame does not wait
  for its list (3b's question 6).
- `node scripts/test-changed.mjs` refuses nextest's `--status-level` flags; the `split_tests`
  rig's `Half::collect` ignores `unread` once `hold` is off (`mapped()` calls it with
  `usize::MAX`).
- Build nothing while a GPU run is up: a rebuilt binary under a running nextest fails its tests
  in milliseconds (read at `53a29dbf`).

## Architect's ruling on Phase 2c's stop (2026-11-25)

> Written by the architect, not `dev`. Read from the code at `b13311a2` and from Phase 2c's
> timelines (`live/p2cd/`, the quiet ones: `radeon-drained-B-q2-*`, `radeon-drained-A-c-*`,
> `radeon-drained-A-q2-a`) and Phase 2b's (`live/p2bd/`); nothing was built or run. Each claim
> says whether it is read from code, read from a log, or derived. The fits are least squares over
> rows the rig already prints, taken by the architect with a throwaway script. The rulings are in
> the plan's amendment of the same name.

### 1. The code

- **The cap** (`bake/queue/step.rs`, `open_step`, lines 294 to 300): `SampleCost::cap(slice +
  edit_ms)` while a dig waits or is open, taken before the step's digs open and before its
  read-backs are collected (the `submit` stage, which runs after it; `read_stats`, 505 to
  510). So a row's
  cap is priced by the row before's price; the price a row prints is the one after its own
  read-back.
- **What the estimate is fed** (`read_stats`): `d.samples` and `d.gpu_ms`, where `d.samples`
  is at least 4 096. `SampleCost::read` (`bake/budget.rs`, 214 to 217) returns on 0 samples.
- **`SubmitDone::samples`** (`bake/job_loop.rs`, line 565) is `report.samples`' growth: the
  samples kept. Not kept and still drawn: a withdrawn job's rounds (lines 503 to 517, counted
  in `report.wasted`), a round past its face's stop and a round of a read-back that failed to
  map (lines 529 to 532). `gpu_ms` (567 to 569) is the whole dispatch's.
- **`class_samples`** (lines 23 to 27, 521, 534, 570 to 581): the class is `OpenJob::own_count`
  (670 to 672), whether the job draws a count of its own. A dig job's is set by whether the near
  tier's count differs from the settings' (`bake/queue/digs.rs`, 262 to 263, 371), the same for
  both rings. `class_ms` is `ms * n / all`: in one dispatch both classes read the same ns.
- **The withdrawal at a dig** (`take_boxes`, `digs.rs` 47 to 55): every job but a key-only one
  in a ring-1 chunk, and every dig's job in any ring chunk. `JobLoop::withdraw`
  (`job_loop.rs` 163 to 208) keeps an open job with rounds in flight in `withdrawn` until they
  are read back. `open_now` (`digs.rs` 412 to 456) withdraws for a slot only for ring 1.

### 2. A sample's cost by the job it is drawn for (log, fitted)

Each row's `flight r1 / r2 / other` is the composition of the submit it sent (they sum to
`sent` in every row); the next row's read-back is its time. Read-backs kept whole (read at
least 0.98 of sent, 4 096 or more):

| runs | read-backs | fixed ms | ring 1 ns | ring 2 ns | other ns | R2 | rms ms | one price | its R2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| quiet B, 8 (`B-q2-*`) | 709 | 0.805 | 14.64 | 16.35 | 7.95 | 0.940 | 0.41 | 0.543 + 15.83 | 0.878 |
| drained A, 8 (`A-c-*`) | 838 | 0.751 | 12.09 | 16.34 | 8.90 | 0.933 | 0.37 | 0.550 + 14.73 | 0.870 |
| quiet A fit run (`A-q2-a`) | 105 | 0.765 | 12.63 | 16.54 | 10.61 | 0.925 | 0.40 | 0.633 + 15.17 | 0.888 |
| Phase 2b B after, 8 (loaded) | 750 | 0.665 | 15.12 | 16.56 | 9.77 | 0.916 | 0.50 | 0.706 + 12.12 | 0.786 |

Submits of one class over 150 000 samples, quiet B: ring 1 alone, 22, 199 257 to 222 255 in
3.06 to 4.59 ms (median 3.67); ring 2 alone, 122, 198 309 to 323 238 in 2.81 to 5.79 ms (median
4.17); other alone, 18, 204 565 to 245 726 in 2.30 to 3.46 ms (median 2.81). Drained A: ring 1
alone, 8, 224 270 to 237 525 in 3.02 to 3.70 ms (median 3.23). **Why** a ring-2 sample costs
more than a ring-1 one and twice any other job's is not read: the read-back carries its rays
and their length (`job_loop.rs` 535 to 536) and no row prints them; Phase 2d (d) does.

drained line's read-backs, other jobs' work (the row before the dig: 0.226 ms + 10.6 ns), and
spent on ring 2 at 16.3 ns. Phase 2b read the same excess where its ring 2 opened. Rule 3's
figures price it from the second dig of a session on (they are kept between digs); at a
session's first dig the D+1 cap is taken before any dig read-back is in, and the D+2 cap has
ring 1's figure from D+0's read-back.

### 6. Why the figures are all in (derived)

A class's ns net of the fixed part needs the fixed part that held when it was read. The general
price's reads 0.000 to 0.47 ms through quiet B and 0.39 to 1.16 ms through A's dig rows; one
ring-1 read-back a dig, read against a fixed part off by 0.8 ms, moves a 4.0 ms cap by a
quarter. All in, a class's figure read at one grant is exact at that grant (the cap it gives
costs the grant: `fixed + per x cap = ms` whatever the split), and a dig's submits all go out
under `slice + edit_ms`. Where the slice changes between a class's read-backs (the view fills
again under a dig) the figure is off by the fixed part's share: a larger grant underdraws, a
smaller one overdraws by at most the fixed part, ruling 2's limit of the Phase 2b ruling. A
three-class fit kept live was not taken: four unknowns over 64 read-backs with a class absent
most of the time.

**The first reading sets the figure** and later ones move it a fifth: with one read-back a dig
and 0.4 ms of spread in a submit's time, a cap that follows the last reading alone would swing
between 183 000 and 276 000 on quiet B's ring-1 submits (4.0 ms over 14.5 to 21.9 ns all in).

### 7. The withdrawn rounds (code and log)

The rule is Plan 0098's and is right: a dig job's rounds in a chunk of the new dig's ring read
the world before the edit; kept, the job's stamp stales it at its end. Phase 2c changed what is
in flight at the next dig (ring 2 of the dig before, where Phase 2b had other jobs' rounds,
which the edit does not withdraw outside a ring-1 chunk): 5 read-backs of 0 a run against 2.
Their 3.8 to 5.4 ms is inside the grant of the frame that sent them, not added to a dig's
frame. The dig lines of every quiet B run read "every ring chunk D+52, 44, 36, 28, 20, 12": no
ring chunk of any of the six digs lands before frame 54, 12 frames after the last dig, and
the rows between two digs (run 1) are 200 000 to 227 000 dig samples a frame at 2.78 to 5.15 ms. How
much of that is withdrawn by the next dig is not on the row (`dig_withdrawn_samples` is in the
stats, `bake/queue/stats.rs` 116 to 117): Phase 4 (5)'s reading.

### 8. Question 7's figures (derived from section 2)

4.0 ms buys 218 200 ring-1 samples by B's fit and 268 700 by A's; 4.5 ms (option (e)) 252 400
and 310 100; 6.0 ms (option (c)) 354 800 and 434 200. The largest ring read at a dig, 275 022,
costs 4.83 ms by B's fit and 4.08 by A's. The rings within 5 % of 4.0 ms: B's digs 3, 4 and 6
(3.81, 4.11, 3.99 ms) and A's dig 3 (4.08).

## Phase 2d: the estimate reads what was drawn, and a dig's cap is priced by its class

Code at `0cfd6e13`. `SubmitDone::drawn`, `dig_drawn` (ring-1 dig jobs', other dig jobs'),
`rays` and `ray_len`, counted over every round of a timed read-back, a withdrawn job's and a
done face's among them; `JobClass` on `Job` and `OpenJob` (`open_digs` sets it from
`DigChunk::near`); `DigCost` in `bake/budget.rs`; `BakeQueue::priced_cap` in `bake/queue/step.rs`
(the cap's class: a `near` dig in `digs` or `dig_open` is ring 1, else any `dig_open` entry is
the dig's, else the general price); `read_stats` feeds both estimates with the drawn count,
read-backs under 4 096 drawn still skipped. The rig's row gains `drawn`, `ray`, `r1` and `dig`.

Rig: Phase 2c's (`dig_line::reading::dig_timeline`, device-wait stepper), binary
`live/bin/p2d/gpu_outdoor` (release, `0cfd6e13`); `live/p2d-dig.sh`, the batch
`live/p2d-batch.sh` (each run its own hold of the exclusive perf lock, waiting in it up to 5
minutes for load1 under 1.5), the parser `live/p2d-check.py` (summaries `live/p2d-{B,A,W,R}.txt`);
timelines and logs `live/p2dd/`. Adapters by `SVET_TEST_ADAPTER`.

## Architect's ruling on Phase 2d's stop (2026-11-25)

> Written by the architect, not `dev`. Read from the code at `243c1f2b` and from Phase 2d's
> timelines and summaries (`live/p2dd/`, `live/p2d-{B,A}.txt`); nothing was built or run. Each
> claim says whether it is read from code, read from a log, or derived. The rulings, Phase 2e
> and question 7 as restated are in the plan's amendment of the same name.

### 1. What a sample is (code)

- **The count** (`settings.rs`, the Portable profile; `tiers.rs`, `near_count`; `digs.rs`,
  `open_digs`): Portable's tiers are 1 024, 256, 256, and a dig job bakes at the near tier's
  count whatever its band, so 1 024 samples a brick direction, as a job's own count (it differs
  from the settings' 4 096).
- **The faces** (`job_loop.rs`, `JobCtx::open`): a job with its own count on cells of 8 draws a
  direction's samples on `ceil(1024 / 64) = 16` of its faces (all of them where it has fewer),
  `ceil(1024 / drawn)` each: 64 a face on 16 faces, one entry of 64 lanes each (`QUEUE_ROUND`).
  `n_min` and `n_max` are both that count, so `plan` takes every round of a face it reaches and
  a job cut by the cap has its first faces drawn whole and the rest not at all.
- **The rays** (`JobCtx::open`; `bake.wgsl`, `bake_round`): `sky_n = ceil(256 / drawn)` and
  `key_n = ceil(64 / drawn)`, 16 and 4 of a face's 64 samples, the first ones. The sky ray is
  walked only where `d.z > 0` (`cosine_direction` about the face's normal: all of an up face's,
  half of a side face's, none of a down face's); the key ray only where `dot(nf, key_dir) > 0`.
  Both walk `shadowed_styled_walk` to `job.radius` or a hit.
- **The candidates** (`emissive.wgsl`, `draw_candidates`, `candidate`; `pipeline.rs`,
  `KEPT_CANDIDATES`; `frame/baked.rs`, `params.candidates`): every sample that is not a
  key-only job's loops 32 candidates; with an empty table each returns at the header, its
  random number still drawn for the merge. The emitter's shadow ray is cast only where a
  candidate was kept; the read-back's `rays` and `ray_len` count those alone.
- **Derived:** a direction of 16 or more faces costs 1 024 samples: 1 024 candidate loops, at
  most 256 sky walks and at most 64 key walks. The entries, the faces' directions and both ray
  counts are exact on the CPU at planning; a walk's steps are not.

### 2. What the rows show of it (log)

- `ray 0.00` in every row of every Phase 2d timeline: no emitter shadow ray in any submit, so
  no candidate was kept: the 32 loops a sample did nothing in this scene.
- **One submit, six quiet runs** (B's dig 4, D+0, the job of 225 725, ring 1 alone; run 2
  disturbed and run 3's cap 170 357 left out): caps 192 974, 193 760, 198 397, 190 979,
  197 205, 196 547; read-backs 3.090, 3.038, 3.604, 3.396, 3.729, 2.818 ms: 14.3 to 18.9 ns a
  sample on the same first faces of the same job. Dig 2's D+0 submit (127 278 of ring 1 and
  ring 2 to caps of 202 407 to 213 552): 2.665, 3.144, 3.638, 3.270, 2.798, 3.365, 3.197 ms.
- **Between digs** (the Phase 2d log's medians, ring-1 read-backs): B dig 3 20.64, dig 4
  16.25, dig 6 17.46; A dig 2 17.07, dig 3 15.92 ns all in. Whole, at its own median: 205 407
  is 4.24 ms, 225 725 is 3.67, 217 503 is 3.80, 221 595 is 3.78, 275 022 is 4.38. The
  summaries' "whole" column (the D+0 read-back plus the cut at the figure) reads 3.37 to 4.79
  for B's digs 3, 4 and 6 less run 2, and 2.99 to 4.86 for A's digs 2 and 3.
- **Not shown by any row:** the sky and key rays a submit drew, so nothing in the logs says
  whether the time follows the rays, the entries or the samples. Other jobs read 8 to 11 ns a
  sample against the digs' 15 to 21; their tier is not on the row either.

### 3. Ring over cap, Phase 2d (log, derived)

| dig | ring 1 | caps met, quiet runs | ring over cap |
|---|---|---|---|
| B 3 | 205 407 (two jobs) | 185 984 to 199 826 | 1.03 to 1.10 |
| B 4 | 225 725 | 170 357 to 198 397 | 1.14 to 1.33 |
| B 6 | 217 503 | 181 545 to 208 889 | 1.04 to 1.20 |

Phase 2c's 14 (its ruling's table): 1.00 to 1.13. An eighth (question 7's (e)) carries none of
Phase 2d's dig 4 and missed one of Phase 2c's; 1.5 is over every quiet row, by 1.13 at the
least.

### 4. Why Phase 2d's caps are smaller than Phase 2c's (code and log)

`priced_cap` prices a step with a ring-1 dig by `DigCost::ns(Ring1)`: ring 1's figure, else the
dig's. At B's dig 3 ring 1 is unread (dig 1's and dig 2's D+0 submits are mixed), so the price
is the dig's figure, 20.02 to 21.51 ns (ring 2 all in, the withdrawn submits of 22.9 ns
counted now), against Phase 2c's general price of 18.65 to 19.98. At digs 4 and 6 it is ring
1's figure, set by dig 3's read-back at 20.2 to 23.5 and moved a fifth by dig 4's. In A ring 1
is never read before dig 3's cap, so both digs take the dig's 17.55 to 18.86.

### 5. The first dig (log)

D1+0: cap 349 012 to 479 483 at other work's 8.3 to 10.9 ns, 4.93 to 6.03 ms. D1+1: 6.33 to
7.81 ms. D1+2: 6.07 to 6.83 ms (its cap is taken before D1+1's read-back is in). From D1+3 on
the dig's figure prices the dig's submits (median 4.02 to 4.14 ms a run). The figures are kept
between digs; no run has a dig after a pause, so whether that holds a later dig's D+0 to D+2
to the grant is not read (Phase 2e's sequence C).

## Phase 2e: ring 1 goes whole in the edit's submit within a stretch of the cap

Code at `74f1d2cd`. `BakeQueue::ring1_stretch` (default `DEFAULT_RING1_STRETCH` 1.0, clamped to
`RING1_STRETCH_RANGE` 1.0 to 2.0); the render setting `bake.ring1_stretch` (Live) through
`Frame::set_bake_ring1_stretch`, which goes into the mailbox as `set_bake_digs` does; the
fly-through's `--bake-ring1-stretch X`; the HUD's queue row `; stretch X` and the banner's
`digs +3 ms, stretch X,` whenever it is not 1.0. In `submit`, after the ring-1 jobs are opened
and promoted and before `dispatch`, `ring1_cap` (`bake/queue/step.rs`) takes the samples the
**open** ring-1 jobs have left to plan (`JobLoop::open_samples_left`; a ring-1 job still pending
for want of a slot is not counted, since no round of it could be planned): over the cap and at
most the stretch times it, the cap is that count. `QueueStats::stretched` records it. The rig:
`SVET_LINE`'s seventh part is the stretch (the file name gains `-sX`), each row ends `stretched
0|1`, sequence `C` (B's dig 1, then B's dig 4 alone 64 frames later; the tail's early break now
applies to the last dig only, which changes nothing in A or B, whose gaps of 1 and 8 end first),
and the rig-only arm token `cand0` (`set_bake_queue(|q| q.settings.candidates = 0)`, no setting
or flag). Rigs: `live/p2e-batch.sh`, `live/p2e-dig.sh` (copies only a timeline written after its
own start), `live/p2e-check.py`, `live/p2e-gpu.sh`; binary `live/bin/p2e/gpu_outdoor` (release,
`74f1d2cd`); timelines `live/p2ed/`. Adapters by `SVET_TEST_ADAPTER`.

## Phase 4: the bake thread keeps the slice fed

### Phase 4's states to Phase 4c and the first feed readings (the resume notes of 2026-11-25, kept as readings)

**State (updated at Phase 4c's end).** Phase 4c is in as `d95e99bb` and `11840dc5` (one
submit a frame, `THREAD_DEPTH` 3 picked by reading, the round-trip line); its readings in "Phase
4c" below. **Its stop fired:** the 16 table slots (`QUEUE_SLOTS`) hold the feed (submits 0.59
to 0.60 a frame at every depth, the freed steps sending nothing with every slot held). Phase 4d
waits for the architect's ruling on it; nothing past 4c is built.

**State at Phase 4b's end.** Lever (b) is in as `514c9a71` (wip); Phase 4b (the
pooled opening's level, the fill, the quiet fix) is in as `5c518cf0`, its readings in "Phase
4b" below. Next is Phase 4c (one submit a frame, the thread's depth): the 4b runs read the
feed held by submits a frame (0.52), not by the drawn share (0.95 to 0.97). Levers (c) and (d)
are not built.

**State at the checkpoint before 4b.** Lever (b) is in as `514c9a71` (wip). Levers (a), the in-flight bound, (c) and (d)
are not built. The checkpoint was taken at the agent's context budget, not at a stop. Phase 3b
is not built and no dig reading was taken with the thread on. Nothing in this phase needs 3b.

**The order the stages come in** (Phase 3a's thread-on RTX forest, `live/p3ar/rtx-forest-on-1.log`,
the bake thread's `queue step B` stages, p50 ms): list 4.99, collect 1.90, checks 1.68, submit
1.51, candidates 1.01, fades 0.62, summaries 0.25 (movable p50 11.8). The bake step (about
12.3 ms) was longer than the frame (7.5 ms), so list went first, as lever (b).

**The real underfeed, read with a new parser** (`live/p4-fed.py LOG...`). It works over the
once-a-second lines while in view waits: submits a frame, drawn p50 / granted per submit, and
their product, "fed a frame". The `bake queue slice:` line's drawn/granted is per submit, so
it hides frames that had no submit. Phase 3a's 0.967 is per submit. The numbers:

| run | submits/frame | drawn/granted a submit | fed a frame | all-slots-unread steps | in view 0 s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3a RTX lockstep (`p3ar/rtx-forest-off-1`) | 1.000 | 0.993 | 0.991 | 0 | 31.4 |
| 3a RTX thread (`p3ar/rtx-forest-on-1`) | 0.417 | 0.980 | 0.422 | 187 | 24.5 |
| 3a RENOIR thread (`p3ar/renoir-forest-on-1`) | 1.000 | 0.985 | 0.985 | 0 | 12.8 |
| (b) RTX thread, 2 runs (`live/p4r/b-*`, load 1.79/1.22 at the lock, quiet) | 0.775, 0.767 | 0.450, 0.487 | 0.363, 0.393 | 430, 431 | 26.9 (both) |
| (b) + listings not counted, experiment (`live/p4r/x-*`, load 3.44 at the lock, **loaded**) | 0.592 | 0.980 | 0.581 | 446 | 18.4 |

These are all RTX Floor 1080p forest fresh-start runs with the thread on (except the lockstep
row), 7200 frames, binaries `live/bin/p4b` and `live/bin/p4x`, under the exclusive perf lock.

- **(b) works on its own stage.** The bake step's movable p50 went from 11.8 to 2.5 ms, list
  from 4.99 to 0.014. The fade share went from 0.61 to 0.91, so a step now comes nearly every
  frame. But each submit drew only 0.45 of the cap: `jobs` read 4 to 5 open, and the slice
  line read "out of rounds" 651 to 1443, against 84 to 367 before (lockstep listed whole
  batches in the step).
- **Why.** `open` counts the pool's candidates at their `listing_bound` (an upper bound) in
  `left` and against `2 * cap`. The opening then stops early, and the faces come back a step
  later. The experiment, not committed, counted the pool's candidates as 0. QUEUE_CHECKS (16)
  bounded the sends a step. `jobs` read 25, drawn per submit 0.98, fed a frame 0.58, and in
  view 0 came at 18.4 s (loaded). The step was longer again (movable p50 5.1 ms: more jobs,
  more collect and submit).
- **What is in the way next.** Two things: the admission, which is lever (a)'s stage, and the
  in-flight bound. Steps with every submit slot unread rose to 446. `IN_FLIGHT` (2,
  `bake/job_loop.rs`) spans the thread's round trip: issued at the take, mapped a frame or
  two later, read in the next message. The in-flight bound is not a named lever. Phase 3a's
  gotchas route it here, and it changes no bytes, tier or slice. If it is taken, it goes in
  Notes as a deviation: a deeper bound with the thread on only. It must be checked against
  the readback buffers in `bake/issue.rs`.

**Lever (a), as the amendments set it (not built).** Done-when (1) wants each step to admit
until the newly opened jobs' projected samples cover the slice, one job of overshoot: 0.4 ms
jobs under a 4.0 ms slice give 10 or 11, under a 1.0 ms slice 3 or 4. Today's rule admits
until `left >= 2 * cap`, two slices of `samples_left`, which gives 20 on an empty loop. The
price is `SampleCost`, the general price (amendment after Phase 2e). "At the frame rate
read" is open to interpretation; it may be the slice times the frames since the last step.
Keep some bound on the backlog, or the pool's allocations and the pending list grow. Decide
how the pool's candidates count in it: their bound over-counts (above), 0 under-counts.
`open` is at clippy's `too_many_lines` limit, so new logic goes in helpers. That is how
`list_batch` was added.

**Not read yet:** the hills (`--source outdoor --time 17:00`), the RENOIR with (b), done-when
(4)'s bars quiet, (5)'s readings (the whole-residency drain, `second:150,0.25`, the bake
thread's busy share and runnable threads against the logical cores with the `taskset` arm, the
ten steps after the view drains, the RENOIR drained B `dig_withdrawn_samples` reading, which
is lockstep: a dig rig), and `gpu_queue,gpu_bake` plus the soak test
`the_threaded_queue_drains_to_the_lockstep_store` by name (it now runs the pool, through
`frame/bake_thread.rs`).

**Gate state at `514c9a71`.** Quick: 594 passed, 24 skipped. `--clippy` 0, fmt 0, every
`scripts/check-*.mjs` 0. New test: `bake::queue::split_tests::lister::the_listing_pool_drains_to_the_blocks_the_step_lists`
(two device-less rigs, the blocks bit-identical by chunk). GPU binaries not yet run.

**Rigs.** These live under the untracked `live/`:
- `live/p4-fly.sh NAME cpus FLAGS` (env `LOGDIR`, `BINDIR`) and `live/p4-batch1.sh` (env
  `TAG`, `PASSES`, `BINDIR`; RTX forest thread on).
- `live/p4-fed.py`, `live/p3a-parse.py` for the columns.

**Gotchas.**
- `pgrep -f '[p]4-batch1'` returns the nohup subshell first, so wait on the `p4-fly.sh` PID.
- Lockstep keeps the scoped listing threads, so its order and bytes are unchanged. The pool
  starts in `Frame::start_bake_thread`'s path (`frame/bake_thread.rs`) and stops at
  `join_bake`.
- `take_box` drops a listing the pool holds, and a result is matched by its sequence number.
- 4b: `take_listed` now runs in `open_step` ahead of the gate, every step; `open` no longer
  calls it. The pooled level is `lister::POOLED_LEVEL` (3), the guard `POOL_GUARD` (64).
- The four pooled tests sleep on the pool (`BakeQueue::wait_listed`, test-only) between steps.

- (1) `the_opening_admits_until_the_projected_samples_cover_the_slice` passes (0.19 s). The
  candidates are lone-voxel chunks of 53 bricks at 625 a brick, 198 750 samples each, prepared
  masks held, so the bound is exact: a lone voxel's brick lists six directions of one face
  each, and a job's samples are a direction's count times its faces. **A deviation from the
  brief's 200 000:** no chunk lists exactly 200 000 with an exact bound (a brick's exposed
  directions sum to an even number, and a count that is not a multiple of 64 is exact only on
  one face a direction, six a brick; 200 000 is not 6 k times a count). The price is the
  brief's 2 ns with no fixed part (the cap 2 000 000 at 4.0 ms, 500 000 at 1.0 ms), reset every
  step. Read: the first opening sends 31 (30 x 198 750 is under 6 000 000), in the walk's

### Phase 4c: one submit a frame, and the thread's depth in flight

**What landed.** `d95e99bb`: `render_frame` replays the carried ops and the taken steps' ops up
to and with the first submit (`replay_a_frame`) and carries the rest in order on the link; the
join puts the carry ahead of the steps not taken (`carried_first`), which the next lockstep step
replays as before. The bound a step's submit reads is `BakeQueue::in_flight_bound`: `IN_FLIGHT`
(2) without the listing pool (lockstep, the pre-roll, the one-shot bakes), the thread's depth
with it (`THREAD_DEPTH`, set by `Frame::set_bake_depth` at the thread's start; the fly-through's
`--bake-depth N`, 1 to 8, a reading's knob listed in `NOT_SETTINGS`, not a settings row). The
render side counts each submit's frame of issue against the frame whose message carried its
read-back, the frames that carried a submit and the issuer's peak readback buffers (teardown
`frames: bake round trip:`); the slice line gained `(K with no table slot free)` after `out of
rounds`, `of S` steps after the blocked steps and, in `11840dc5`, `wasted samples W of D drawn`.
Rig: `live/p4c-batch.sh` (env `PASSES`, `DEPTHS`), parser `live/p4c-parse.py LOG...`, binary
`live/bin/p4c3` (built at `11840dc5`), archive `live/p4cc-tests.tar.zst` (at `d95e99bb`).

**The done-whens.**

### Step 4: the held block as the step writes it (done-whens 4 and 5)

The wip read a held record as one carrying the stale mark (bit 17 of the second sky word, word
8). Wrong, from the code: `apply_held` writes a held cell's sky words with `VIS_BAKED` and no
`SKY_STALE`, the key words cleared, all six directions in the mask; `fill_directions` sets all
six on a touched kept brick. And `gpu_queue`'s dig rig bakes no sky visibility at all (words 7
and 8 zero), so neither the mark nor a held visibility exists there: its only trace of the
dig's block is the mask going 0x30 to 0x3F. First run of the wip (lavapipe, exclusive lock,
load 23): lockstep's own edit frame failed the oracle.

`support::queue::dig_held(pre, cur)`: against the pool read after the edit and before any step
read its box, every record that held light has all six directions, every baked visibility is
stale or held-shaped, and one brick changed or was dropped. `ring1_landed(held, cur)`: no
record stale and the records differ from the edit frame's. `dig_timeline`'s pool line reads the
same from its own pre-step read. Two later misses, both in lockstep, fixed in the oracle (not
the claim): a re-dig (sequence B's sixth) drops bricks the step no longer lays out; a dig over
an open ring (B's fifth) finds every record already stale, the held cells written over them.
After each fix lockstep's held read D+0 on every dig (validated on one RTX A and one RENOIR
drained B lockstep run before the batch).

Pacing. Unpaced, a threaded frame of the rig costs about 0.1 ms: ring 1 read D+10 to D+36
threaded against D+1 lockstep, though in ms it landed with lockstep (10.7 to 26.8 ms against
11.1 to 16.0). Paced to a fixed 16.667 ms: D+2 against D+1 at load 1.2, but D+5 inside the gpu
tier's parallel run (load 8 to 20, the contended lavapipe step outrunning three frames). The
rig now pads each threaded frame to lockstep's mean frame on the same dig (from its edit's frame
to ring 1, at least 16.667 ms). The claim and the bar are unchanged; the architect may rule on
the pace.

| run | adapter | lock, load1 | held | ring 1 lockstep / thread | wall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| wip as committed | lavapipe | exclusive, 23 | lockstep fails (oracle) | D+0 / D+0 (oracle) | 8.0 s |
| fixed 16.667 ms pace | lavapipe | exclusive, 1.2 | D+0 both, bit-identical, 3 digs | D+1 (33.6 ms) / D+2 (50.5 ms) | 7.9 s |
| same, gpu tier parallel | lavapipe | exclusive, 8 to 20 | as above | D+1 / D+5: failed | 30.7 s |
| lockstep's frame pace, nextest gpu | lavapipe | exclusive, 3.4 | passes | passes | 8.7 s |

6.5 s alone under nextest (load 1.05), under the 10 s soak line: in the gate, not marked.

## Architect's ruling on Phase 3b's stops (2026-11-25)

A code and log reading at `0779f503`; nothing was built or run. The rulings are in the plan;
this is what they stand on.

### 1. The held block never told (code, then the timelines)

- `writes.rs`: `ListMaker::finish` returns the edit's list first; `Lists::take` inserts an
  edit's list after the leading edit lists, ahead of every landing list waiting; `Lists::apply`
  pops from the front. `link.rs`, `render_frame`: a box's frame calls `mapped`, `send`, then
  `take_waiting(frame)`, which returns every entry in the outbox up to the first made from
  that frame's message; the loop then calls `lists.take` per step in the order made.
- So on the edit's frame the lists of the step before the dig's (it ran after the last
  frame's take, so its entry is still in the outbox) are applied after the dig's edit list.
- `store.rs`: `show` writes a fading block's shown words at the block's place; `put` writes
  the block and the chunk table's word; `digs.rs`, `advance_fades`: every step shows every
  running fade; `take_boxes` removes the dug chunk's fade and puts the held block at the old
  place when it fits (`len <= words`), else at a new one.
- `pool+0` on every row of `rtx-waiting-B-thread-*.txt` and `radeon-drained-B-thread-*.txt`:
  no place was refused.
- `rtx-waiting-B-thread-1.txt`: frame 33 (D4+7) lists `[-1, 9, 8]`, `[-1, 9, 9]`,
  `[-1, 10, 9]`, `[0, 9, 8]`, `[0, 9, 9]`, `[0, 10, 9]` LANDED; dig 5 at frame 34 opens ring-1
  jobs in `[0, 10, 8]`, `[-1, 10, 8]`, `[0, 9, 8]`, `[-1, 9, 8]`: two of its ring-1 chunks
  landed one frame before, a dig's fade of 4 frames running.
- `radeon-drained-B-thread-1.txt`: `[0, 9, 8]` LANDED at frame 15 (D2+5); dig 3 at frame 18
  opens ring-1 jobs in `[0, 9, 9]` and `[0, 9, 8]`: the fade is still shown by the step made
  from frame 17's message. Digs 2, 4, 5 and 6 dig `[0, 9, 9]` alone, whose ring 1 landed
  6 frames before each (frames 4, 20, 28, 36): no fade running. Dig 6's `[0, 9, 8]` landed at
  frame 41 and is not a ring-1 chunk of it.
- Not shown by the timelines: RTX dig 3 (`[-1, 10, 8]` LANDED at frame 17, dug at 18) and dig
  4 (`[-1, 10, 8]` RING 1 LANDED at frame 23, dug at 26) are told at D+0 with a fade running.
  The reader takes the block's place from the books (`read_store_block`), so a held block put
  at a new place reads held whatever is written to the old one. No row prints a place.

### 2 and 3. The wait and the call (code, then the rows)

- `thread.rs`: the loop pushes a step's entries after `step` returns; `link.rs`, `bake_step`:
  the entry is the step's ops, lists, stats and `Books::of`. `take_until` ends on
  `books.frame >= frame`. `Recorder::poll` is empty.
- `Books::of` under `trace_digs` (the timeline sets it) also builds the dig trace and clones
  the coarse set, on every step, inside the wait.
- The dig step's stages over 0.5 ms, thread on (`D+0 stages`): RTX waiting B run 1, digs 1 to
  6: 7.2, 10.2, 11.6, 10.8, 6.3, 5.5 ms against waits of 8.5, 13.1, 15.7, 12.7, 12.2, 10.0.
  RTX waiting A dig 3 (one frame after dig 2): submit 16.9 and 11.7 ms, waits 35.6 and 31.2;
  lockstep's p99 on the same dig is 45.0. RENOIR drained B: 3.1 to 3.7 ms against waits of
  5.3 to 5.5; lockstep's whole step 2.6 to 4.5.
- The column: `step.rs`, `apply_lists` (`total_ms`, the step) and `bake_thread.rs`,
  `thread_frame` (`total_ms` and `render_ms[S_APPLY]`, the call's wall).

### 4. Ring 1 (code, then the rows)

- `link.rs`: `mapped` polls and collects without waiting, before the frame's own issue is
  mapped; the read-backs leave in `send`. `step.rs`, `submit`: the collect places them; the
  step's lists leave at its end.
- `rtx-waiting-B-thread-1.txt`, dig 5: D+0 issues two submits; D+1 and D+2 take no step (the
  row repeats the stats, `read 0`, call 0.46 and 0.07 ms); D+3 reads the two back. The step
  made from D+1's message was still running through two of the rig's frames.
- Ring 1 fresh, thread on: RENOIR D+2 in 104 of 104 read; RTX A D+3 8, D+4 4, D+5 3, D+6 1;
  RTX B D+2 1, D+3 22, D+4 15, D+5 1, D+6 1. (8 digs a rig are dig 2 of sequence A, overtaken
  by dig 3 a frame on, and 8 a rig the digs of section 1.) Every slot unread at the dig: RTX A
  2 of 24, RTX B 4 of 48, RENOIR 0.
- No row holds the ms from the dig to ring 1.

### 5. The submits on a dig frame (rows)

RENOIR drained B: two 43, three 5. RENOIR waiting A: two 16, one 8. RENOIR waiting B: two 48.
RTX waiting A: three 12, two 4, one 8. RTX waiting B: three 34, two 11, one 3. Lockstep: one,
always.

### 6. The pace

`gpu_queue.rs`, `dig_reads`: every frame from the edit's on is padded to `frame[k]`,
lockstep's wall from the edit to ring 1 over its frames on dig `k`, 16.667 ms at least; the
held records are compared before any padding matters. The digs are made after the chunk has
settled and 16 frames more: no fade runs at any of them.

## Phase 3c: an edit's list lands after the writes made before it

Code at `4686656e`. A step's lists keep the order made (the landings before the box, the edit's,
the landings after; a unit over the maker's cap as a list of its own in its place, never
carried); `Lists::apply` takes every list up to and with the last edit's whatever the cap, then
landings while they fit, and keeps the bytes over the cap (`Lists::over`). The maker switches
lists on its `editing` flag at the next sealed unit, so `step.rs` (outside Files touched) is
unchanged. `Frame::read_store_block` takes the place from the pool's chunk table word, read
back with the window in the pool's header (`Frame::read_table_word`), and the words from the
books where the places agree, else the directory's run: every caller, not only the dig readers.

### The done-whens

| done-when | reading |
|---|---|
| (1) `an_edits_list_lands_after_the_writes_made_before_it` | passes, 0.009 s: a show and the edit's block at P under a cap of 68 bytes (64 each) leave P the edit's; the table reads Q; four landings of 524 160 bytes and a 512-byte edit under 1 MiB apply 2 MiB, `over` 1 048 576, the two landings made after wait and apply next frame (200) |
| (2) `a_steps_lists_keep_the_order_they_were_made` | passes, 0.008 s: lists (landing 4, edit 36, landing 32, forced 512) in that order; the table word reads the edit's |
| (2) `an_edit_list_is_never_carried`, restated | passes, 0.006 s: 660 in the edit's frame, `over` 560, the landing behind it waits |
| (3) the wait test's fading case | passes, 0.42 s with the rest of the test: chunk 0's ring 1 fading, the step before the dig's held, both writes at chunk 0's place in the edit's frame, the place the pool's table names holds the store's held block. **Fails on the old `writes.rs`** (checked: the same test with `writes.rs` at `2f389883`, "chunk 0's place holds the held block after the edit's frame") |
| (4) lockstep bit-identical | `the_threaded_queue_drains_to_the_lockstep_store` passed (12.4 s, lavapipe, exclusive lock, load1 9.65 at the lock, 5.39 at the end: loaded); `--profile gpu` gpu_queue gpu_queue_reach gpu_bake gpu_sun gpu_settings_rows 83 passed (lock load1 0.64, end 3.30); gpu_golden gpu_outdoor_golden gpu_castle_golden gpu_hud_golden 25 passed, no golden moved (load1 8.36 / 3.57, loaded). From the archive `live/p3c-tests.tar.zst` at `4686656e` |
| (5) held at D+0 | 48 of 48 digs, below |
| (6) apply lap on the dig frames | RTX p50 0.287 ms, max 0.566; RENOIR p50 0.049, max 1.013 (dig 1 of run 1); the stop (1.0 ms p50 on the RTX) did not fire |
